# T6040 GPU parity audit

Audit baseline: `integration/aurora` at
`9cbdd0734d9e`, with GPU integration #463 (`a0a9485847f8`), the
r5 display series (`905dff774561`), camera #462 (`2114ed0c6245`), and the
two r6 display patches. The reconstructed r6 branch is
`wip/j616s-display-r6` at `af0a664046fb`.

This series repairs regression coverage and a shared KUNIT=m WERROR build defect,
ports GPU-driven memory/fabric demand and PMP voter cooperation using
T6040 data, honors the existing DT idle-delay policy, and closes a compute
release-witness gap across recovery. GPU voltages, firmware features and the submission algorithm remain
unchanged from the reconstructed base. The major
T6040 calibration and image-admission changes requested by this audit are
already in the baseline. Software parity does not establish all-workload benchmark or CTS
parity. The operator has selected g3 as the measured J616s baseline; g4
retains it and adds the qualified shared ports and diagnostics below.

## GPU memory demand and interactive latency

The GFX PMP report entry and PMC performance votes are separate mechanisms.
The existing T6040 `pmp_gfx` soc-device 13 entry follows the powered GPU domain;
`apple_pmp_report_set_state()` publishes a power-state request bit and waits
for its acknowledgement. It does not encode a DCS/fabric performance request.
The GPU firmware owns the PMP's performance demand. Before this series, PMC's
static voter list omitted PMP agent 0, and CLPC lacked a GPU power-state input.
The two existing ports enable PMP agent 0 on T6040's DCS/SOC rails and make
CLPC request the already qualified DCS 5 / fabric 2 floor. G3 incorrectly
used the T6040 supplier lifetime as activity. G4 replaces that input with
DT-selected publication/continuous-idle demand and preserves the existing
32 ms memory-floor release. Prove live
request, enabled-agent and actual-state agreement; supplier binding alone
is not proof of an honoured vote.

For dispatch latency, G17 now reads the existing `apple,idle-off-delay-ms` DT
policy, validates the existing 2..1000 ms bounds, and starts the existing host
hold with that policy. J616s selects 16 ms, matching the existing effort-idle
interval; other boards without the property retain the native 2 ms behavior.
This avoids power-off across short interactive gaps and releases after real
idle, without changing firmware layout or adding a parameter/platform flag.
KUnit covers absent/valid/invalid policy and repeated depth-one gaps followed
by idle release. A shorter software delay is not itself a measured latency
win: compare actual power transitions, firmware execution, IRQ/retirement and
fence wakeup boundaries. Keep empty CPU fence transfers separate from GPU
round trips and require low-duty/long-idle power acceptance.

## Remaining 25G83 facts and recovery qualification

No firmware capability is inferred from another GPU's successful CTS result.
The following are the exact T6040/25G83 facts needed to open the remaining
paths. A reference capture of achieved frequency establishes a target, not
an offset, voltage, descriptor ABI, or Linux residency measurement.

| Gated path | Exact facts needed for T6040/25G83 | Current evidence and disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sustained top clock | DT OPP count, both voltage lanes, hardware A/B selector maps, selected logical-state limit; independently decoded achieved frequency or residency, counter units/timebase/wrap, request versus achieved-state semantics and limiting reason under sustained load | Per-chip DT handoff and maps are already mandatory; both maps select the final raw OPP. `max_frequency_khz` is an advertised maximum, not achieved telemetry. G17 `stats_snapshot()` returns None; g4 raw `firmware_reports` and `asahi_g17_report` preserve payloads but establish no decoded residency. Keep voltages/maps unchanged and require a measured Linux load trace. |
| Raw versus logical state count | Meaning of the ADT count properties for this firmware, both SGX map banks and number of entries actually consumed by the 25G83 power tables | Public `HwData25G83`, calibration validation and loader handoff select eleven logical states from sixteen raw OPPs. Do not resize the firmware structure from the ADT property name or discard its unsorted OPPs. Compare actual top selection and achieved clock. |
| DVFS ramp / idle / fast-die | 25G83 meaning and units of ramp/effort controls, idle/off delays, temperature limits and hysteresis; PMP/AP/CLPC request ownership, achieved floors and release under idle and load | Existing shared submission/idle policy, DT suppliers and T6040 power-state graph are enabled. Power-state presence is not proof of load tracking. Require live requests, actual floor release and thermal traces; opaque power words are not tunable parameters. |
| Recovery slot attribution | Exact 25G83 slot-key storage range, lifetime, cache policy and slot stride; key QID/stamp bit fields; slot-mask meanings, progress monitor semantics and association with an actual started render | `recovery_slot_keys=None`; never copy T8140's physical address. Preserve firmware blame. Progress-view reads no longer depend on knowing the key address; bounded diagnostics report unknown ownership explicitly. |
| Recovery table / reason | 25G83 recovery-info offset/extent, valid/guilty/cancelled bits, stamp sequence encoding; reason-code semantics; ordering and reset of records relative to states 1/2/3/0; interrupted-command drain and visibility guarantees | Existing checked 256-entry table and handshake remain unchanged. Reason 3 is treated as page fault by existing attribution. New generation/slot diagnostics aid qualification; missing key evidence cannot clear blame. An aged compute record may no longer substitute for a stamp across recovery. |
| Compute completion / visibility | Exact compute record identity and timestamp fields, stamp write/cache-maintenance order, ring consumption during restart, late/missing-stamp behavior and a release witness for interrupted work | Same-generation 50 ms fallback retained. Crossing recovery now requires a covering stamp for ordinary retirement; quarantine, kill/drain and processor-stop ownership remain. A completion record plus elapsed time after restart is not independent visibility evidence. |
| MMIO pair registration | 25G83 MMIO port addresses and access-power witness; pair registration write order, ACK and coexistence with lead commands/bootstrap QID reservation | Keep disabled. The 26A428 sequence, POWER_ON_DONE message and recovery-target pointer are distinct ABI facts. |
| MCache | 25G83 table-entry format, sizes/alignment, descriptor pointers and enable bits, ownership/coherency rules, partial-render/restart behavior | Keep disabled until a licensed public implementation or independently supplied facts establish the whole contract and tests validate it. |
| Few-primitives | T6040 enable-bit semantics, required auxiliary buffer sizes/registers and partial-store/load/resume handling for all tiling/fragment paths | Keep disabled; previously observed hangs are not grounds to enable it for performance. |
| Ray traversal / preemption | T6040 register program, scratch sizing/alignment by enabled cores/clusters, saved operand-state layout, resume/checkpoint semantics, kill/restart contract, scheduler timer mask and priority profiles | Existing two-by-ten operand layout and priorities retained; periodic timer mask remains zero on both variants. Native traversal remains disabled pending bounded execution/fault/recovery qualification. |
| TLB range operations | T6040 opcode/operand encoding, VA/ASID granules, ordering, completion and negative-translation invalidation across every mapping alias | Existing page TLBI and PoC table cleaning retained. Copying Neo range encoding cannot close a performance gap safely. |
| Large vertex/barrier and tess/geometry faults | Faulting engine/access/VA/VM, exact bound intervals/backings and mapping lifetime; render/compute descriptor identity, TVB/GTP capacities, pass-start/stamp state and recovery reason before acknowledgment | Isolate exact public CTS names with one process and compare kernels with the same Mesa. Buffer size alone does not establish VM-bind, timeout or TVB causality. Retention fix is independently justified, not a claim to have cured a CTS fault. |

The compute stamp-wait KUnit suite now checks that an aged exact record and
consumed items cannot retire a pre-recovery kick without its stamp, while an
exact/later stamp, busy-ring control and same-generation fallback retain their
expected behavior. Recovery-source tests cover unknown keys, incomplete reads
and page-fault fallback. Diagnostic counters explicitly identify lifetime
stampless totals; they are not a count of commands retired by one restart.


## g5 large-target capacity correction

Public CTS `vulkan-cts-1.4.6.2` uses a 10240-square framebuffer for
`rasterization.primitive_size.points.point_size_10000`. The existing 25G83
GTP layout appends a sub-buffer of 64 times the tile map after the TPC.
Applying the inherited 32 MiB cache bound to this combined allocation rejects
that valid target with ERANGE, despite its cache and tile record each fitting
their original limits. This is reproducible in the production layout code
without GPU execution. The normalized hardware summary contains ERANGE and
reason-2 recoveries but cannot establish their order or one-to-one association.

G5 applies the original TPC_MAX to the aligned cache, preserving the original
tile-record bound before allocation or growth. The tile-map bound also caps
the derived auxiliary extent. It changes no firmware buffer-sizing formula,
numeric limit, VA window, generation/retention rule or allocation accounting.
Neo has no auxiliary tail and keeps the same policy. KUnit covers both utile
shapes at the CTS target size, oversized cache/layered-record rejection and
Neo capacity. Fresh and growing targets use the same preflight. Large targets
need their full required metadata through the existing memory/VA mechanisms;
allocation failure still propagates. Hardware execution/readback and post-close
memory/capacity baselines are mandatory acceptance gates.

The separate 1 MiB vertex/barrier case uses a 256-square target, not a large
framebuffer. Its point count is actual vertex-buffer bytes divided by stride.
It cannot be explained by the large-target capacity guard. Preserve its first
operation/exception, generated sequence and the halted-state TVB/growth/pass
snapshots before choosing a coherence, VM-bind or firmware-timeout fix. The
point-target correction is not a claim that firmware reason 2 is decoded.

## g5 recovery evidence and host-hop timing

The shared recovery service previously captured slot keys and monitors at
halted state 1, then called `render_pass_started()` during classification
after acknowledging state 3 to 0. A resumed render could change that pass
state. G5 computes attribution from the state-1 pass snapshot and carries
only that result through the handshake. KUnit drives the real service and
rejects any pass read after the first acknowledgement. Unknown 25G83 keys
still retain firmware blame. This is an independently demonstrated ordering
fix; it does not establish the cause of either deterministic reason-2 fault.

G5 adds the existing declared TVB state (inventory, page capacity, producer,
consumer, high-water and reset) to the halted snapshot without resetting any
counter. The blamed pair's first four commands include render geometry and
the computed GTP layout, alongside the existing pass/cursor/stamp records.
Pending/active growth and its reply remain visible. The next isolated run
can distinguish exhausted inventory or delayed growth from a pass that never
started; no buffer enlargement or firmware timeout change is justified yet.

Static-key `asahi_g17_inputs` records compute input conversion entry/result,
source count and publication waits. `asahi_g17_schedule` records enqueue,
inline/fallback, preparation and run boundaries. Together with the existing
publish/complete, DMA-fence and scheduler tracepoints these split host cost
per hop into input resolution, scheduler setup/wait/dispatch and firmware
execution. They make no timing claim until a matched trace is captured.

The available macOS reference uses 26A428 on T6040 and reports a software
request P10 while hardware residency is 1578 MHz at raw state 14. Linux runs
the admitted 25G83 image. Thus neither a literal P10 conversion nor copying
another firmware's statistics layout proves achieved MHz. The reference
contains aggregate frequency/residency and public DT/calibration facts,
not a Linux 25G83 tag/field decoder. The inspected licensed public G17 source
at `jgoyvaerts/linux` `cc32473a09dd` also has no decoded statistics adapter.
Required input remains a fresh Linux raw report series under labeled idle,
ALU and fill load, and independently established field/timebase/achieved-state
semantics for that exact firmware. No captured raw payload has been promoted
to public implementation data. Achieved clock/power and the CTS cure remain
open; g3's measured memory/latency policy is preserved.

## g4 shared runtime ports and observability

The operator has selected g3 as the hardware baseline. This follow-on retains
its CLPC/PMP ports, DT idle hold and recovery-generation release guard.
Individual hardware records remain in the private qualification workspace;
no raw lab capture is a public implementation source.

| Gap | Public source behavior | T6040 g4 disposition | Qualification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| #504 fragment notification | `de0b22a8370e` selects a render pair on its fragment notification; final retirement still requires both stored stamps | Included for both firmware graphs; KUnit covers each mask bank and unrelated queues | Render-light latency/IRQ rate, growth/partial renders, recovery and CTS |
| #504 tiling first-kick | `5073eac15192` changes the word only for the 26A428 register program | Included unchanged; 25G83 retains 1 on every kick | No T6040 notification-halving claim; requires 25G83 descriptor/notification facts and controlled qualification |
| #496 compute-fault witness | `9a76756bf2d7` uses event 7 words 3/4 as QID/stamp on Neo, with consumed-ring guard | Imported prerequisite, but the existing firmware graph keeps 25G83 event 7 unknown | Exact 25G83 layout, queue identity, stamp semantics and DMA/ring visibility must be established before it can release memory |
| #505 closed-context teardown | `078d744a260c` kills before freeing executed unstamped render graphs, retains firmware-linked work storage, and reuses killed pairs last with QID capacity | Shared safety/retention paths included; compute-fault-driven kill depends on the graph-qualified #496 witness | Closed mixed compute/render faults, acknowledgement ordering, memory pressure and slot/QID reuse; does not claim to cure a live render's first fault |
| #499 long-job sharing | `3db24da5c830` recognizes another published, unobserved long compute job as sharing, without changing numeric renewal bounds | Included in the shared host path; T6040 bounded AS-build classification can use it, while native traversal remains gated | KUnit verifies renewals stop at the existing bound; no claim that this cures a firmware reason-2 render fault or makes skipped traversal CTS pass |
| First fault | Reason and blame alone cannot identify engine progress | At halted state 1, before acknowledgement, g4 logs the blamed pair's stored stamps, free-list balance and up to four commands' pass/cursor snapshots; borrowed pairs are explicitly unavailable | Correlate exact CTS operation and original error with raw event report and snapshots; do not label reason 2 as page fault without semantics |
| Raw firmware statistics | Complete 18-word statistics records already have checked ring geometry | Read-only `firmware_reports` exposes last full sample, count and host monotonic time for each role/type. Static-key `asahi_g17_report` copies trace/statistics/event words before slot return | Match payloads to independently decoded achieved-clock/power/residency and freshness/units; legacy statistics tags are a different ABI |
| Idle memory floor | Neo graphics PMGR follows activity; T6040 GFX is held for firmware lifetime | g4 replaces T6040 PMGR demand with a DT-linked CLPC request, asserted at publication and released by the existing race-checked 16 ms continuous-idle clear; 32 ms CLPC hold remains | Read-only last vote/reason/time, host request and last submission, safe core power/host hold; require blank-panel quiet release and load/release cycles |

Remaining exact facts for telemetry are the 25G83/26A428 tag-to-layout map,
field width/signedness/endianness, sample/timebase/wrap and freshness,
power units/averaging interval, logical/raw/cluster pstate selection, requested
versus achieved-state semantics, and limiting/thermal reason meanings. Raw
samples and the DT maximum are not MHz. Reads issue no firmware request and
never touch gated core registers. `agx_stats` remains unsupported for decoded
G17 data until those semantics are established.

For the two deterministic CTS cases, retain the first failing operation before
cleanup, full unnormalized recovery logs, raw event trace, and pre-ack render
snapshots. The large-buffer and extreme-point-size cases can stress tiling,
but neither case name proves a TLB, timeout or TVB cause. No timeout, voltage,
feature-enable or buffer-size guess is included in g4. Multi-queue sync needs
its publish/GPU-span/event/fence timeline; #504's compute control does not
promise to halve that path's latency.

### T6040 idle-floor release correction

The PMGR DT explicitly documents that GFX cannot power down while firmware
runs. This is a source-established difference from the Neo CLPC input, so
using GFX as T6040 work demand can never establish idle release. G4 removes
that input on T6040 and adds `apple,clpc` from the GPU consumer to the existing
host performance controller. This is a provider relationship, not a feature
flag or user knob. A requested but unavailable provider defers GPU probe.

Publication asserts the independent, refcounted request through a leaf lock;
o sleeping callback or allocation runs at submission. Both mutex-backed and
off-lock direct paths are covered. The existing effort clear checks in-flight
work and 16 ms continuous idle, withdraws its flag, then rechecks the direct
submission counter. A racing publisher reasserts demand. The CLPC vote keeps
its existing 32 ms hold; CPU arbitration and Neo's PMGR input are unchanged.
Firmware stop and request destruction clear demand. Provider removal cannot
free storage retained by a late consumer, and a device link orders dependencies.

CLPC status now shows the last requested floor, reason bits and monotonic
time, plus the host request's activity/update sequence. Actual PMC readback
and write errors remain distinct. Read-only `asahi-g17/power` reports the
existing always-accessible core power witness, host hold, effort and last
publication time, without waking firmware. Generic runtime PM may remain
unsupported because autonomous core gating is firmware-managed; no fictitious
runtime-suspend implementation is enabled. Raw stats still do not decode MHz.

The production CLPC host suite tests an always-powered supplier with absent,
busy and ended GPU demand, 32 ms hold, CPU coexistence, reason/time and legacy
Neo behavior; removing demand or idle release fails its negative controls.
C KUnit checks actual request lifetime and a late consumer after provider
removal. Hardware acceptance requires a locked/blank panel with publications
observably quiet, release after the hold, then three load/idle cycles and
retention of g3's benchmark gains. An active compositor alone proves neither
quiescence nor a latch. Keep failed measurements and rollback candidates.

## Source and validation boundaries

The implementation references are the licensed public Aurora/Asahi kernel and
loader history. Existing Gravity Linux-derived layouts and attribution remain
unchanged. The public Gravity Linux `gravity-m4` reference inspected at
`4fc326f6c25ccceb7515e2635c22e01fde5d3bac` was checked in `g16.rs`,
`g16_runtime.rs` and `g16_power.rs`. Those files do not supply validated
T6040 recovery slot keys; that G16 runtime explicitly leaves compute
non-preemptible. No private implementation, firmware bytes, voltage table or
lab record is included here.

The build handoff must identify the exact tested source and loader, verify boot
payload checksums, retain r6g rollback, and distinguish CPU/QEMU tests from GPU
execution. Missing J616s measurements are not passing results.

## Neo KMD improvements on T6040

This ledger audits refinements and handling, not just feature parity. It uses
`integration/aurora` at `9cbdd0734d9e186c5f5f5949a21b909d10c2571b` and
`aurora-wip` at `927a4a4480cf90ce8e3b4ab1d1ce58d84bf32fd4`. The conservative
history window starts 2026-09-26 (Neo platform prerequisites), before the public
T8140 firmware interface of 2026-10-03 and hardware bring-up of 2026-10-06.
Committer dates retain rebased older-author changes. The inventory also includes
the complete 105-commit public #171 history, all 65 commits of #423, and the
related PR versions used to identify original contributions and later ports.

The path log covers `drivers/gpu/drm/asahi`, Apple PMP/PMGR/PMC/CLPC/MCC,
RTKit/mailbox, CPU-frequency/thermal and DART code, related AIC and DRM
scheduler/fence support, Rust DRM interfaces, and Neo DT dependencies. Broad
path/PR neighbors and imported baseline changes are explicitly marked N/A;
they are not represented as measured Neo optimizations. Old/rebased hashes are
listed as encountered aliases of the same subject/class, not asserted to be
byte-identical. The coverage inventory lists 1,551 physical commits (including
imported neighbors and PR-original versions), with 851 distinct subjects; all
65 #423 commits have separate rows. **Active** describes the surviving mechanism on T6040, including
its 7.3 port/refinement, rather than claiming that every historical SHA is an
ancestor. Multi-purpose firmware-introduction records retain their shared
parts while explicitly identifying dormant MCache/native-feature portions. A commit link is its exact diff; the evidence key states the current
reachability/gate. No per-commit speedup is invented from a stack measurement.

### Applicable ports found by the commit audit

| Neo mechanism | T6040 in g1 | g2 port and per-chip data | Required acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| #440 GPU-driven CLPC memory/fabric floor | CPU demand only; `gpu_demand=false`, no GPU power-state phandle | `0d63308820ab`: existing GPU-demand mechanism and DT phandle, T6040 DCS 5/fabric 2 and existing 32 ms release; CPU sustain=3 retained | C/R/H/I/P; actual PMGR tracking, floor release, video/browsing and sustained-load power |
| #219 PMP voter cooperation | Static voters 1/3 omit PMP 0 from pre-working-PMP bring-up | `772a9d3104ff`: existing `apple,pmp` supplier dependency, voters 0/1/3, existing T6040 DCS/SOC rail mask; no display rail or voltage change | P/B; PMP bound/ready, live request/honoured floors, idle and thermal behavior |

Other audited gated paths are listed explicitly below. The T8140 lazy CPU
verification cost is absent on T6040's already-fast-switch profile. MMIO pair
registration, range-only TLB encoding, native RT/few-primitives and optional DCS
cooling cannot be enabled by copying a different firmware/hardware protocol.
The recovery slot-key attribution gate still needs validated 25G83 identity
facts; shared recovery, deadlines and retirement handling remain enabled.

### Reported measurements and their scope

These are public PR results, not new J616s measurements. Private qualification
reports were checked for scope and regressions; raw lab records are not copied
here. The older GPU profiling report's cost estimates are not measurements.

| Evidence | Relevant commits/mechanism | Reported Neo result | J616s confirmation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [#171](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/171) | Retention, off-lock cleanup, reclaim quiet window and shared lifetime stack | Cold first-submit median 224 to 194–198 us, p99 338 to 295–305 us; menu-launch stalls below 100 ms versus 260–280 ms before quiet-window work. Stack results, not isolated gains. | B/M/C/R, repeated fresh contexts and foreground launch under bounded pressure |
| [#171](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/171) | Completion/recovery isolation | Earlier mixed render/compute lost its device within 2 s; later fixture ran over 568k verified checks in 60 s. Historical 1 ms stamp fallback was subsequently refined to 50 ms. | F/H/T; exact data and fences, peer liveness and no premature mapping release |
| [#423](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/423) | Cumulative scopes and submission stack | Same-boot submit API elapsed roughly 14 to 10 us; throughput stayed near 20 us. Combined candidate C-d64 19.44 us, R-d32 41.48 us, hop 42.12 us; workload/Mesa dependent. | C/R/H; preserve wall-time versus thread-CPU distinction |
| [#423](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/423) | `9bf3039873a1`, completion identity index | Scanner self samples 4.87% to 2.24% at depth 64, 3.16% to 1.93% on hops. CPU profile result. | O/C/H |
| [#423](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/423) | `26446182da3a`, 128-interval validation working set | C-d64 API elapsed 10.06 to 7.46 us; R-d32 12.29 to 8.16 us. Not a thread-CPU clock measurement. | C/R/D with rotating 64 buffers and remap/permission invalidation |
| [#423](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/423) | `b6248acf900c`, private-fence fast status | Submit API median 7.52 to 5.79 us; frontier samples 1.83% to 0.25%; throughput remained near 19.5 us. | C/H/O; errors, foreign inputs and delayed host publication |
| [#440](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/440) | `d69f14f4e4fb`, GPU fabric floor | C-d1 158.9 to 108.2 us; C-d64 19.3 to 14.7 us; R-d32 42.7 to 29.7 us; hop 42.2 to 31.9 us. Same driver/Mesa. | P/C/R/H, compare g1/g2 and keep heavy/idle checks |
| [#219](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/219) | GFX report/PMP vote and PMC enables | Short manual-bit glmark2 build experiment: 1453 with GFX report off to 2199 with it on; 2218 with all interface enables. Single-run 7–10 s experiment, not an isolated final-kernel A/B. | P/R; distinguish reported request, enabled agent and honoured floor |
| [#443](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/443) | Lazy verification and fast switch | C-d2 58.8–59.7 to 46.4–47.3 us; C-d1 107.8–108.4 to 102.6–102.7 us. T8140-specific spin removed. | C/I observation only: T6040 already lacks that spin |
| [#413](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/413) | Existing optional idle hold | The roughly 1.1 ms wake and 1.2–1.4 ms tiny-render-after-gap observations are **M4 Pro**, not Neo measurements. Default 2 ms adds no extended hold. | I at defaults; optional hold is a separate policy experiment |

The provided current Neo Linux/macOS report has no completed paired Linux
record, so an overall Neo margin is still undefined. Historical PR ratios do
not establish the requested current all-workload margin. Use matched suite,
Mesa, clock/power conditions and repeats. Run only workloads admitted by the
controller; queue depth is not permission for extra process workers.

### Reachability and gate evidence keys

| Key | Current source/gate and interpretation |
| --- | --- |
| SHARED — Shared G17 execution | driver.rs selects g17::Gpu for T6040. g17/queue/mod.rs, runtime.rs, port.rs, runtime/render/direct.rs and runtime/render/submit.rs have no T8140-only gate on these mechanisms. The variant supplies QID floor 32, producer +0x50, two-cluster operands and 25G83 lead commands. |
| DRM — Shared DRM/scheduler/fences | G17 queues use job.rs, fence.rs and the common DRM scheduler/syncobj/GPUVM/shmem interfaces. CPU_FREQ and UCLAMP_TASK are enabled in the release config. Private cumulative scopes are advertised on both G17-backend targets at feature bit 29; foreign fences retain generic handling. |
| MEM — Common memory and lifetime | file.rs::memory_stats, gem.rs, memacct.rs, cleanup.rs, pgtable.rs and mmu.rs serve the T6040 G17 backend. Creator accounting, pins, revocable backing, canonical bindings and deferred destruction are not gated to T8140. T6040 keeps its explicit UAT coherency policy. |
| GMEM — Shared retained GPU resources | g17/freelist.rs, context.rs, object.rs, queue/compute.rs and runtime/render/pools.rs retain and reclaim the same resource owners. Allocations use T6040 core-layout sizes and its reduced QID budget; no Neo-only admission branch disables them. |
| REC — Shared recovery and deadlines | g17/recovery.rs, recovery/safety.rs, timeout.rs and runtime/events.rs serve both firmware variants. T6040 uses its status-B base and one firmware role. Exact retirement, interrupted-work failure, withheld-doorbell replay, progress-aware budgets and sticky VM reporting remain active. Current missing-stamp fallback is 50 ms, not the historical 1 ms. |
| POWER — Shared feed/effort/idle code | g17/feed.rs is instantiated for T6040 and derives CPU floors from each discovered policy maximum. g17/mod.rs::note_submission/idle_check and power.rs share effort 1.0 while work streams and a 16 ms idle clear. The existing optional idle hold is available on T6040 but its default 2 ms adds no extended hold. |
| TIMESTAMP — Shared timestamp publication | timestamp.rs::FirmwareBuffer retains a CPU vmap for the buffer lifetime; g17 completion publishes through it before signalling. T6040 uses this shared firmware-buffer path and its 24 MHz timestamp conversion, not a per-command vmap. |
| PRIORITY — Shared firmware priority profiles | g17/mod.rs::scheduling_policy selects HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW profiles for both targets; file.rs checks CAP_SYS_NICE or DRM master for HIGH and refuses REALTIME. Priority work-ring selection and QoS ownership are shared. |
| FW — Per-chip firmware adaptation | hw/t6040.rs selects V25G83. g17/initdata/v25g83.rs supplies one merged root and status acknowledgement, per-chip OPP/calibration input, separate views and no POWER_ON_DONE. Pointer and operand layouts are selected from Config/Variant rather than copied from the two-instance 26A428 graph. MCache is explicitly gated by V25G83.mcache=false; its firmware table/protocol is not part of admitted T6040 support. |
| PMGR — Shared PMGR state handling | pmdomain/apple/pmgr-pwrstate.c binds T6041 states through the t8103-compatible state protocol. BUSY masking, timeout unwind and firmware-owned/no-auto-PM properties apply through DT; no T8140-only register address is copied. |
| PMP — PMP/report per-chip data | soc/apple/pmp.rs and pmdomain/apple/pmp-report.c already contain T6041 match data: 42-bit DMA, version-5 bootargs checks, CoreAnalytics EP7, T6041 report offsets and three fast-die lanes. T6040 DT supplies the bootargs/report entries and CPU effort phandles. T8132/J713-only telemetry policy is excluded separately. |
| PMC — Shared PMC, T6040 rail mask | pmdomain/apple/pmc.c::apple_pmc_probe/agent_enable use T6040 agent stride, DCS maximum 5 and agent_rails=GENMASK(1,0). CPU/CLPC/AP voting already worked. Port 772a9d3104ff adds apple,pmp and static voter 0 after its supplier binds; display rails remain excluded. |
| CLPC — Shared CLPC, T6040 demand data | soc/apple/clpc.c uses T6040 DCS {1,2,5,5}, fabric {0,1,2,2}, sustain=3, 4 ms sampling and 32 ms hold. CPU demand/lifecycle refinements are shared. GPU demand was missing and is enabled by 0d63308820ab with the existing apple,gpu-power-state phandle. |
| GPUFLOOR — Applicable gate ported | Before g2, apple_clpc_hw_t6040.gpu_demand was false and its CLPC DT node had no apple,gpu-power-state. Port 0d63308820ab enables the same mechanism as #440 with T6040 DCS 5/fabric 2, not Neo 4/3. Hardware gain and idle/PMP cooperation remain unqualified. |
| MCC — MCC translated to 16 planes | soc/apple/mcc.c accepts the T6041/T8140-compatible controller; T6040 DT describes 16 planes and its display boot-DMA dependency. The J616s enables this node. Generic partial-enable unwind, lifetime and SLC allocation rules apply with that data. |
| CPU — Shared CPU frequency/limit handling | T6041 CPU nodes bind the t8112 fallback profile. Generic OPP cleanup, fast-switch and PMP effort QoS use its DT frequencies and three effort lanes. No Neo frequency or thermal curve is copied. |
| LAZY — Different CPU verification protocol | The lazy_verify refinements are T8140-only. T6040 binds soc_t8112_info, whose verify_transition is false; policy->fast_switch_possible is already true. It does not pay the T8140 blocking verification loop that these commits remove. Adding lazy verification would not port a missing gain. |
| DART — Related DARTs; native GPU uses UAT | iommu/apple-dart.c common publication/error/resume/stream logic is used by T6041 DARTs where their DT ownership mode selects it. T6040 display mirroring uses its own compatible/data. Native GFX translations use asahi/mmu.rs UAT, so this is related PMP/display robustness, not a claim that Neo DART addresses fit the G16 GPU. |
| RTKIT — Shared RTKit transport/lifetime | G17 Coprocessor uses the Rust RTKit wrapper over soc/apple/rtkit.c. Shared buffer retention, barriers, crash handling, bounded logs and shutdown ownership apply. T6040 supplies its firmware-data crash buffers and one ASC instance; MTP/J713-only adoption is excluded. |
| MBOX — ASCWrap transport, per-chip registers | soc/apple/mailbox.c and rust/kernel/soc/apple/mailbox.rs provide the common queue, IRQ and receiver ownership rules. T6040 binds its ASCWrap-v6 data; its wrapper/VBAR windows and IRQs come from DT. Neo-specific mailbox addresses are not reused. |
| AIC — Shared effective-affinity reporting | irq-apple-aic.c applies AICv2+ reporting to the T6041 AIC3 compatible too. It reports actual effective routing rather than manufacturing CPU migration. Record the IRQ/worker CPU on J616s; no isolated throughput gain is claimed. |
| DISPLAY — Related shared display hardening | Generic apple/dcp.c, parser and AFK lifecycle/bounds fixes are inherited by the reconstructed display stack where those helpers are used. They protect the display consumer of GPU buffers; they are not standalone compute performance claims. |
| OBS — Observability only when selected | Existing G17 tracepoints/diagnostics and first-failure logging are shared. Trace register sampling is conditional on the tracepoint being enabled; disabled tracing is not a new runtime policy or a measured throughput gain. |
| MMIO — Different registration ABI | runtime/render.rs::install_pair returns before registration power holds when V25G83.mmio_pair_registration=false. 25G83 installs from queue configuration and uses lead commands; 26A428 uses MMIO pair registration. Do not add its two control round trips or register writes to the admitted 25G83 protocol. |
| FEW — 25G83 rejects this fast path | V25G83.few_primitives=false; command.rs and feature reporting enforce it. The supported-source comment records a fragment hang with the flag. It is not an applicable refinement of the admitted 25G83 render ABI without new validated facts. |
| RT — RT specialization not admitted | V25G83.ray_traversal=false; command.rs rejects native scratch/intersection programs and get_params omits the feature. Generic watchdog/recovery infrastructure remains shared, but native RT pools/kill guards/RT-specific long-job use are dormant on the admitted 25G83 path. |
| KEY — Missing validated recovery identities | hw/t6040.rs has recovery_slot_keys=None. recovery/attribution.rs falls back to firmware blame when slot identity evidence is incomplete. Neo slot-key addresses must not be invented for 25G83; this specific attribution refinement remains gated pending validated T6040 facts. |
| UAT — Qualified alternate invalidation policy | hw/t6040.rs keeps reserved_tables, clean_tables and page_tlbi=true. mem.rs::tlbi_range takes the page/ASID alternative before its range encoder. The range-only field fix is dormant; per-SoC coherency/ownership support is active. Removing these working firmware-table requirements is not a justified performance port. |
| DCS — Optional cooling actor, no T6040 curve | PMC cooling/IPA paths require #cooling-cells and power-model DT data. J616s does not select them, and the Neo DCS thermal zone was subsequently removed because it throttled unrelated GPU/CPU heat. No T6040 power curve is invented to re-enable that policy. |
| DCSOFF — Already equivalent inactive zone | Neo removed its oscillating DCS thermal zone. J616s has no such zone to remove. Its PMP CPU/GPU limits remain; this does not justify disabling thermal protection. |
| IRQENTRY — DT-selected report context | IRQ-safe report entries are selected by apple,irq-safe for PMGR-parent use. GFX report consumption does not require copying the external-display parent topology; use the actual T6040 entry/consumer hierarchy. |
| STAT — Not the Neo backend | StatsChannel/GpuManager agx_stats decoding belongs to the maintained backend. g17::Gpu::stats_snapshot returns None on both Neo and T6040. It is not a Neo optimization waiting to be ported to G16S. |
| G15 — Different backend/hardware | T6030/T6031/T8122/G15 and M3-only runtime commits do not execute in T6040 g17::Gpu. Their firmware records, generation-specific power policy and native-G15 admission cannot be copied into G16S/25G83. |
| G16 — Different G16 backend/owner ABI | T8132/J713 uses the separate g16 backend and retained-owner PMP/TVM protocol. T6040 is G16S but dispatches to the g17 backend with V25G83, not that G16G runtime. Match/board gates and firmware ownership differ. |
| LEGACY — Maintained G13/G14 path | The workqueue/manager/versioned AGX firmware path is not T6040's G17 backend. G13/G14 automatic preemption, timestamp-copy and old InitData changes are not missing G16S mechanisms. |
| OTHERCHIP — Other CPU/guest hardware | T8142/T8152/T6050-specific transition, ownership or guest-timer data is not selected by T6041 CPU/GPU match data. No unsupported register/profile change is ported. |
| TUNNEL — Other peripheral topology | M1 tunneled PCIe/Thunderbolt recovery is not a Neo native-GPU improvement. T6040 has its own topology; these records are retained for coverage, not presented as a gated G16S optimization. |
| PERIPH — Not a GPU refinement | This scoped-history/PR neighbor concerns NVMe, HID, audio, SEP, USB, RTC or another peripheral, not a Neo native-GPU performance/handling mechanism. No port is implied. |
| BOARD — Neo-specific board route | The literal J700 topology/display source/domain-cell fix is not T6040 board data. Its independent J616s display/PMP/GPU composition is retained; copying a Neo route or physical address would be wrong. |
| RAWDIAG — Superseded raw diagnostics | The raw T8140 PMP DVFS debugfs dump was later removed. Current reporting uses validated per-SoC fields; no new raw interface or platform knob is added. |
| OLDTHERMAL — Superseded thermal policy | The interim J700 SMC CPU policy was removed/replaced by thermal-framework and PMP handling. It is not the policy selected for T6040 and no Neo temperature/power curve is copied. |
| SUPERSEDED — Superseded inside the series | The unconditional-doorbell/unbound-inline policies were refined by later #423 commits; the early disabled-J616s admission was replaced by calibrated #463 enablement. Current code preserves low-pressure probes and defers/coalesces eligible doorbells with explicit release/stall/recovery handling; do not reapply the obsolete intermediate policy. |
| VERIFY — Build/test/documentation only | This record changes tests, descriptions, bindings, layout/build checks or source style rather than a runtime performance mechanism. Its contract is retained where applicable; there is no isolated hardware speedup or new platform behavior to port. |
| MERGE — Integration record | This merge is provenance/composition, not an isolated performance mechanism. Its parent changes are enumerated below, including variant preservation and subsequent fixes; no separate speedup is assigned to a merge. |

### Hardware measurements referenced by each commit

All hardware actions require the controller's explicit J616s identity, policy
approval and ownership/session/resource gates. These are a measurement plan,
not permission to bypass them. Pair g1/g2 with the same loader and Mesa and
preserve failed runs, logs and r6g rollback.

| Code | Measurement |
| --- | --- |
| C | compute.light d1/d2/d64; submit API wall and thread-CPU time, scheduler hops, faults and CPU-frequency/IRQ context; matching Mesa/suite. |
| R | render.light d1/d2/d32 and 8 queues, first render, heavy fill and tiler growth; API p50/p99, throughput and memory high-water. |
| H | same/cross-queue binary and timeline hops; p50/p95/p99, doorbells per command and 100k order/data checks; include eligible and fallback dependencies. |
| I | idle.wake after 2/20/100 ms, default policy, first-frame latency and clocks; 60/120 Hz frame pacing and idle power. Keep optional hold experiments separate. |
| F | controller-approved bounded stall/fault/long-job stress with innocent peers; no wrong data or false successful fence, recovery phase/time, VM errors and post-close reuse. |
| M | multi-client create/close/rearm and approved bounded pressure; committed/resident bytes, retained pools/QIDs, restoration time and leak slope. |
| P | PMP readiness, PMC per-agent requests/honoured floors and CLPC readbacks during GPU-only, mixed CPU/GPU, 90 s heavy load, idle, video and browsing; verify idle release and no sustained thermal regression. |
| T | timestamp/query correctness, 24 MHz conversion, aligned/unaligned publication where supported, buffer-map reuse and query CPU cost. |
| Q | privileged/unprivileged priority admission and concurrent LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH progress, global-priority transition cases and timeout attribution. |
| D | approved bounded VM_BIND/unmap/remap and DART ownership/resume boundaries; CPU cost, invalidation errors and zero unexpected DART/UAT faults. |
| B | exact boot/kernel/loader/Mesa pins, firmware admission, first queue/install, panel/display regression, supplier readiness and rollback. |
| O | existing tracepoints/counters under approved instrumentation; IRQ and event-worker CPU, pass duration, notifications and failure site. Tracing off remains the reference. |
| V | static/build/contract or capability-refusal check; no isolated hardware improvement claimed. |

### Public measured follow-ups outside the audited branch heads

[#464](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/464),
[#468](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/468) and
[#494](https://github.com/aurora-silicon/linux/pull/494) were also read. Their
heads are not in either pinned branch snapshot; they are not silently counted
as inherited or qualified ports. They remain separate proposals for review:

- #464 limits the T8140 GPU floor to streams and yields to PMP demand. Its
  reported video power is 5.151 to 4.975 W, with light-submit gains retained.
  Check the same video/browsing/heavy-load failure modes on g2, including actual
  T6040 PMP voter behavior, before accepting its unconditional powered floor.
- #468 reports 7409 to 4625 doorbells in a traced hop, slow hops 19.6% to 13.9%,
  and C-d64 13.89 to 13.11 us. Its shared compute-doorbell mechanism would need
  no T6040 feature gate; it is an additional unmerged source dependency.
- #494 reports event passes 8.2 to 6.2 us and submit.empty d2 1.78 to 1.69 us.
  Its shared event housekeeping change is another unmerged source dependency;
  it is not evidence that the audited branches already provide those gains.

No register, voltage, slot-key address, power curve, module parameter or new
platform option is invented to close an evidence gap. Applicability of dormant
25G83-specific feature/identity paths must come from validated facts; successful
compilation is not that qualification.
